(2.35)

Deterministic water waves flow.

Theorem
2.8
below contains all the information used in the probabilistic arguments of the forthcoming sections, concerning
the pure gravity water waves flow for any initial datum
(\eta_{0},\psi_{0})
in the ball
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)
defined in (
1.30
),
provided
s
is large enough and
\varepsilon
is small enough. To give a self-contained proof
we first report the following lemma
stating the equivalence of the norm

\left\lVert u(t)\right\rVert_{K,s}
in (
2.12
) and
the Sobolev norm
\left\lVert u(t)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{s}}
, for a
sufficiently
small solution
u(t)
of the water waves system.

Lemma 2.7
.

(Norm equivalence
[
5
, Lemma 6.3]
)

There is
s_{0}>0
such that for any
s\geq s_{0}
, for all
0<r\leq r_{0}(s)
small enough, there is
a constant
C_{s,K}>0
such that the following holds true:
if
(\eta,\psi)
is a solution of the water waves equations (
1.5
) such that the complex function
u
in (
2.9
) satisfies

\sup_{[-T,T]}\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{K,s}\leq r
, then there is a constant
C_{s,K}>0
such that

\left\lVert{\partial}_{t}^{k}u(t,\cdot)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{s-k}}\leq C_{s,K}\left\lVert u(t,\cdot)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{s}}\ ,\hskip 10.00002pt\forall 0\leq k\leq K\ ,\hskip 10.00002pt\forall|t|\leq T\ .

(2.36)

In particular the norm
\left\lVert u(t,\cdot)\right\rVert_{K,s}
is equivalent to the norm
\|u(t,\cdot)\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}
.

The estimate (
2.36
)
follows directly from the fact that

u
solves the PDE
(
A.9
) in the appendix, whose vector field loses one derivative.

Theorem 2.8
.

(Deterministic water waves flow)
Let
N\gg K\gg s_{0}\gg 1
be the constants provided by
2.4
.
Fix

\delta\in(0,1)
and
s\geq N+\frac{5}{4}
.
For any
{\mathtt{R}}>0
, any
0<\kappa\ll 1-\delta
, there exists
\varepsilon_{0}:=\varepsilon_{0}({\mathtt{R}},\kappa)>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
, the following holds true:

(i)

(Long time existence)
for any initial data
(\eta_{0},\psi_{0})\in{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)
in (
1.30
),
the solution
(\eta,\psi)(t;\eta_{0},\psi_{0})
of the water waves equation (
1.5
) exists up to times

|t|\leq T_{\varepsilon}:=\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)+\kappa}
and fulfills

\sup_{t\in[-T_{\varepsilon},T_{\varepsilon}]}\left(\|\eta(t;\eta_{0},\psi_{0})\|_{H_{0}^{s-1}}+\|\psi(t;\eta_{0},\psi_{0})\|_{\dot{H}^{s-1}}\right)\leq\underline{C}\,{\mathtt{R}}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}\,,

(2.37)

for some
\underline{C}>0
independent from
\delta,{\mathtt{R}},\kappa,\varepsilon
and the initial datum
(\eta_{0},\psi_{0})
.

(ii)

(Birkhoff normal form map)
The corresponding function
u(t;\eta_{0},\psi_{0})
defined in
(
2.9
), with
\upomega
given by (
2.6
),
satisfies the smallness condition (
2.12
)
required by
2.4
,
and so the map

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{t}\colon{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)

\displaystyle\to\dot{H}^{N}\ ,

(2.38)

\displaystyle(\eta_{0},\psi_{0})

\displaystyle\mapsto\Upsilon^{t}(\eta_{0},\psi_{0}):=z(t;\eta_{0},\psi_{0}):=\mathfrak{B}(u(t;\eta_{0},\psi_{0}))u(t;\eta_{0},\psi_{0})

is well defined for any
|t|\leq T_{\varepsilon}
;

(iii)

(Smallness)
For any

(\eta_{0},\psi_{0})\in{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)
,
the functions
u(t;\eta_{0},\psi_{0})
and
z(t;\eta_{0},\psi_{0})
defined in the previous item satisfy

\sup_{t\in[-T_{\varepsilon},T_{\varepsilon}]}\left\lVert u(t;\eta_{0},\psi_{0})\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{N}}+\sup_{t\in[-T_{\varepsilon},T_{\varepsilon}]}\left\lVert z(t;\eta_{0},\psi_{0})\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{N}}\leq\underline{C}\,{\mathtt{R}}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}\,

(2.39)

for some
\underline{C}>0
independent from
\delta,{\mathtt{R}},\kappa,\varepsilon
and the initial datum
(\eta_{0},\psi_{0})
.

(iv)

(Lipschitz property)

There exist
C_{1},C_{\rm lip}>0
such that for any

(\eta_{0i},\psi_{0i})\in{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)
,
i=1,2
, any

|t|\leq T_{\varepsilon}
, we have

\|\Upsilon^{t}(\eta_{01},\psi_{01})-\Upsilon^{t}(\eta_{02},\psi_{02})\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{1}e^{C_{\rm lip}|t|}\,\left(\left\lVert\eta_{01}-\eta_{02}\right\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+2}_{0}}+\|\psi_{01}-\psi_{02}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}+2}}\right)\,.

(2.40)

Proof.

(i)
In view of (
2.7
) and since
the functions
V,B
in (
2.4
)–(
2.5
)
vanish at
\psi=0
,
any initial datum

(\eta_{0},\psi_{0})
in
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)
belongs to the space
X^{s-1}
(cf. (
2.29
)) and

\left\lVert(\eta_{0},\psi_{0},V_{0},B_{0})\right\rVert_{X^{s-1}}\lesssim\left\lVert\eta_{0}\right\rVert_{H_{0}^{s}}+\left\lVert\psi_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{s}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{cB0}}}{{\lesssim}}{\mathtt{R}}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}\ .

(2.41)

Provided
\varepsilon
is sufficiently small, condition (
2.31
) holds and Theorem
2.6
guarantees the existence of a
unique solution
(\eta,\psi,V,B)(t,x)
in
X^{s-1}

satisfying, by (
2.34
) and (
2.41
),

\|\eta(t,\cdot)\|_{H_{0}^{s-1}}+\|\psi(t,\cdot)\|_{\dot{H}^{s-1}}+\|V(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s-2}}+\|B(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s-2}}\leq\underline{C}{\mathtt{R}}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}\,,\hskip 10.00002pt\forall|t|\leq c{\mathtt{R}}^{-3}\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)}\,,

(2.42)

for some
\underline{C}>0
independent from
\delta,{\mathtt{R}},\kappa,\varepsilon
and the initial datum
(\eta_{0},\psi_{0})
.
For
\varepsilon_{0}:=\varepsilon_{0}({\mathtt{R}},\kappa)
sufficiently small, the time

T_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)+\kappa}\leq c{\mathtt{R}}^{-3}\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)}
,
concluding the proof of
(i)
.

(ii)

By (
2.10
), since the function
u
in (
2.9
)
vanishes at
(\eta,\psi)=(0,0)
, the assumption

s-1\geq N+\frac{1}{4}
and
(
2.42
),
imply that

\displaystyle\left\lVert u(t,\cdot)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{N}}\lesssim\|\eta(t,\cdot)\|_{H_{0}^{N+\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\psi(t,\cdot)\|_{{\dot{H}}^{N+\frac{1}{4}}}\leq\underline{C}{\mathtt{R}}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}\,,\hskip 10.00002pt\forall|t|\leq T_{\varepsilon}\,,

(2.43)

proving the first bound in (
2.39
)
(the constant
\underline{C}
may be larger than in (
2.37
)).
Then

2.7
and (
2.43
) imply that

\sup_{t\in[-T_{\varepsilon},T_{\varepsilon}]}\|u(t;\eta_{0},\psi_{0})\|_{K,N}=\sup_{t\in[-T_{\varepsilon},T_{\varepsilon}]}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\|\partial_{t}^{k}u(t;\eta_{0},\psi_{0})\|_{\dot{H}^{N-k}}\leq C_{1}{\mathtt{R}}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}\

(2.44)

for some
C_{1}>0
and so, for
\varepsilon
small enough,

u(t;\eta_{0},\psi_{0})

satisfies
the smallness condition (
2.12
).

(iii)
It follows from (
2.43
) and (
2.13
).

(iv)

We now prove (
2.40
) for any
t\in[0,T_{\varepsilon}]
. By time reversibility the same estimate holds in
[-T_{\varepsilon},0]
.
We rely on the Lipschitz estimates proved in
[
1
]
.
Let
(\eta_{i},\psi_{i},V_{i},B_{i})\in C^{0}([-T_{\varepsilon},T_{\varepsilon}],X^{s-1})
be the solutions of (
1.5
) with initial data
(\eta_{0i},\psi_{0i})
in
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)

provided by item (
i
).
Define

\displaystyle\triangle\eta:=\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}\,,\hskip 10.00002pt\triangle\psi:=\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\,,\hskip 10.00002pt\triangle V:=V_{1}-V_{2},\hskip 10.00002pt\triangle B:=B_{1}-B_{2}\,,

(2.45)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{M}}_{i}:=\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\varepsilon}]}\|(\eta_{i},\psi_{i},V_{i},B_{i})\|_{X^{s_{0}+2}}\,,\hskip 10.00002pt{\mathfrak{N}}(t):=\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\left\lVert(\triangle\eta,\triangle\psi,\triangle V,\triangle B)\right\rVert_{X^{s_{0}+1}}\,,

with
s_{0}
given by
2.4
(which can be taken greater than
6
).
By
[
1
, Formula 5.3]
, for any
0\leq t\leq T_{\varepsilon}
,

{\mathfrak{N}}(t)\leq{\mathcal{K}}({\mathfrak{M}}_{1},{\mathfrak{M}}_{2}){\mathfrak{N}}(0)+t{\mathcal{K}}({\mathfrak{M}}_{1},{\mathfrak{M}}_{2}){\mathfrak{N}}(t)

(2.46)

for some non-decreasing constant
{\mathcal{K}}({\mathfrak{M}}_{1},{\mathfrak{M}}_{2})\geq 0
.
The constants

\mathfrak{M}_{i}
in (
2.45
) satisfy, in view of
(
2.30
), (
2.42
) and
s-2>s_{0}
,
the uniform bound

\mathfrak{M}_{1}+\mathfrak{M}_{2}\leq\underline{C}{\mathtt{R}}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}\leq 1\,,

(2.47)

so the constant in (
2.46
) satisfies

{\mathcal{K}}({\mathfrak{M}}_{1},{\mathfrak{M}}_{2})\leq{\mathcal{K}}(1,1)\,.

(2.48)

For any
t\in[0,T_{\varepsilon}]
where
T_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)+\kappa}
we
divide

[0,t]
in intervals of size
t_{1}
where

t_{1}
is smaller than
the minimum of
t
and
1/2{\mathcal{K}}(1,1)
.
Then, on
[0,t_{1}]
, (
2.46
) and (
2.48
) imply

{\mathfrak{N}}(t)\leq 2{\mathcal{K}}(1,1){\mathfrak{N}}(0)
and, iterating such bound between
[t_{1},2t_{1}],\ldots,[t-t_{1},t]
, we deduce, for some
C_{\rm lip}>0
,

{\mathfrak{N}}(t)\leq e^{C_{\rm lip}t}\,{\mathfrak{N}}(0)\,,\hskip 10.00002pt\forall 0\leq t\leq T_{\varepsilon}\,.

(2.49)

We now prove the bound (
2.40
) for

\Upsilon^{t}(\eta_{01},\psi_{01})-\Upsilon^{t}(\eta_{02},\psi_{02}):=z_{1}-z_{2}
where

z_{i}:=\mathfrak{B}(u_{i})u_{i}
and

u_{i}:=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|D|^{-\frac{1}{4}}\eta_{i}+\tfrac{{\rm i}}{\sqrt{2}}|D|^{\frac{1}{4}}\upomega_{i}
,
i=1,2
, satisfy, by
(
2.39
) and
N\geq s_{0}+1
,

\sup_{t\in[-T_{\varepsilon},T_{\varepsilon}]}\left\lVert u_{i}(t,\cdot)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}+1}}\lesssim{\mathtt{R}}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}\,.

(2.50)

Thus, for any
t\in[0,T_{\varepsilon}]
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert z_{1}-z_{2}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq\left\lVert z_{1}-z_{2}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{lipB},\eqref{massa1}}}{{\lesssim}}\left\lVert u_{1}-u_{2}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{an:u},\eqref{Mi.est}}}{{\lesssim}}\|(\triangle\eta,\triangle\psi)\|_{H^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{4}}\times{\dot{H}}^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{4}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{def:Ns(t)},\eqref{epVBnorm}}}{{\lesssim}}\mathfrak{N}(t)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{lipconst},\eqref{def:Ns(t)}}}{{\lesssim}}e^{C_{\rm lip}t}\left\lVert(\triangle\eta(0),\triangle\psi(0),\triangle V(0),\triangle B(0))\right\rVert_{X^{s_{0}+1}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{an:DNX},\eqref{Mi.est},\eqref{epVBnorm}}}{{\lesssim}}e^{C_{\rm lip}t}\big(\|\eta_{01}-\eta_{02}\|_{H^{s_{0}+2}}+\|\psi_{01}-\psi_{02}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}+2}}\big)

proving (
2.40
).
∎

Part II
Probabilistic formation of rogue water waves

In this Part we prove the Large Deviation

3.1

which directly implies
1.1
, as we show in
Section
3
.
In Sections

4
and
5
, relying on the normal form
long time well-posedness
results we construct better approximate water waves solutions, and
we prove the Large Deviation estimate (
1.26
),
up to the much longer times

|t|\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{5}{2}(1-\delta)+}
,
and
|t|\leq\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)+}
,
as stated in items (
i
) and (
ii
).
The proofs
are rather different in these two cases.
Finally, in Section
5.4
we prove the dispersive focusing mechanism in
1.2
.

3
The main theorem

The following theorem provides the sharp estimate
of the probability that
random initial data of typical size
\varepsilon

will lead to a
rogue water wave with a crest

\geq\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}
.

Theorem 3.1
.

(Large deviation principle)

There exists
\underline{s}>0

such that
for any
\lambda_{0}>0
,
\delta\in(0,1)
, and
s\geq\underline{s}
there exists
{\mathtt{R}}_{0}>0
and for any
{\mathtt{R}}\geq{\mathtt{R}}_{0}
, any
0<\kappa\ll 1-\delta
, the following holds true. There exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
,
for any random initial data
(\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})\in{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)
in (
1.30
),
the solution
(\eta(t,x),\psi(t,x))
of the water waves equations (
1.5
) exists up to times
|t|\leq\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)+\kappa}
, and satisfies
the large deviation principle

\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}\varepsilon^{2\delta}\,\log{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\{\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\eta(t,x)\geq\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}\right\}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)\right)=-\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}}{2{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}^{2}}\,,

(3.1)

with
{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}
in (
1.16
), and for any time
t
satisfying

(i)

|t|\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{5}{2}(1-\delta)+\kappa}
;

(ii)

|t|\leq\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)+\kappa}
provided
\delta\in(\frac{3}{5},1)
.

The rest of this section is dedicated to the proof of
1.1
assuming
3.1
.

We first remark that the assumption

c_{n}=d_{n}\sqrt{n}
in (
1.14
) implies the following lemma.

Lemma 3.2
.

(Random initial data)

Let
(\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})
be a random initial datum as in (
1.13
). Then the Fourier coefficients
(\zeta_{0j}^{\omega})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}

of the complex initial datum
\zeta_{0}^{\omega}(x)
defined in (
1.22
) are independent
complex Gaussian random variables

\zeta_{0j}^{\omega}\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{C}}\Big(0,\varepsilon^{2}\frac{c_{j}^{2}}{|j|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\,,\hskip 10.00002pt\forall j\neq 0\,,

(3.2)

where
c_{j}
are the constants defined in (
1.14
).
In particular the modulus

(|\zeta_{0j}^{\omega}|)_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
are independent Rayleigh r.v.
\sim\mathscr{R}\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\varepsilon|j|^{-\frac{1}{4}}c_{j}\big)
.

Proof.

In view of (
1.18
) and (
1.13
),
the complex initial datum
\zeta_{0}^{\omega}(x)
in (
1.22
) is equal to

\zeta_{0}^{\omega}(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}\underbrace{\varepsilon\frac{r^{\omega}_{j}+{\rm i}s^{\omega}_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}}_{=\zeta_{0j}^{\omega}}\,e^{{\rm i}jx}\,\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{ where }\hskip 10.00002pt\begin{cases}r_{\pm n}^{\omega}=(c_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\,\alpha_{n}^{\omega}\pm d_{n}n^{\frac{1}{4}}\,\delta_{n}^{\omega})/\sqrt{2}\\
s_{\pm n}^{\omega}=(d_{n}n^{\frac{1}{4}}\,\gamma_{n}^{\omega}\mp c_{n}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\,\beta_{n}^{\omega})/\sqrt{2}\,,\end{cases}\forall n\in\mathbb{N}\,.

(3.3)

By assumption (
1.13
),
(\alpha^{\omega}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
,
(\beta_{n}^{\omega})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
,
(\gamma_{n}^{\omega})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, and
(\delta_{n}^{\omega})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
are i.i.d. real Gaussian random variables
\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{R}}\left(0,1\right)
and, by the assumption

c_{n}=d_{n}\sqrt{n}
in (
1.14
),
each
r^{\omega}_{\pm n}
and
s^{\omega}_{\pm n}
is a real
Gaussian distribution
\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{R}}\Big(0,\dfrac{c_{n}^{2}}{n^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)
.
Thus each Fourier coefficient

\zeta_{0j}^{\omega}
satisfies (
3.2
).
Regarding their independence, note that

r_{j}^{\omega}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}s_{j^{\prime}}^{\omega}\ \ \ \forall j,j^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\},\hskip 10.00002ptr_{j}^{\omega}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}r_{j^{\prime}}^{\omega}\mbox{ and }s_{j}^{\omega}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}s_{j^{\prime}}^{\omega}\ \ \ \forall|j|\neq|j^{\prime}|\,.

In addition, the Gaussian variables

r_{n}^{\omega}
and
r_{-n}^{\omega}
are independent if and only if

0=2\mathbb{E}[r_{n}^{\omega}\,r_{-n}^{\omega}]=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,c_{n}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\alpha_{n}^{2}]-n^{\frac{1}{2}}\,d_{n}^{2}\mathbb{E}[\delta_{n}^{2}]\hskip 10.00002pt\Leftrightarrow\hskip 10.00002ptc_{n}=n^{\frac{1}{2}}\,d_{n}\,.

The same condition implies the independence between
s_{n}^{\omega}
and
s_{-n}^{\omega}
. This proves that all the

(\zeta_{0j}^{\omega})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
are independent.
∎

Proof of
1.1
assuming
3.1
.

We now fix
\lambda_{0}>0
,
\delta\in(0,1)
,
0<\kappa\ll 1-\delta
and a time
t
satisfying
(i)
or
(ii)
in
1.1
. Define the event

\mathcal{A}^{t}(\lambda):=\Big\{\omega\in\mathbb{\Omega}\ \big|\ \sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\eta^{\omega}(t,x)\geq\lambda\Big\}\,.

(3.4)

For any initial datum in the ball

{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)

with
s>\underline{s}
given by
3.1
,
the solution
(\eta,\psi)
of the water waves equations (
1.5
) is well defined and therefore

\mathcal{A}^{t}(\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta})\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)=\mathfrak{R}^{t}(\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta})\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)

(3.5)

where the set
\mathfrak{R}^{t}(\lambda)
is defined in (
1.25
).

Lower bound:

In view of (
3.5
) we have

{\mathbb{P}}(\mathfrak{R}^{t}(\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}))\geq{\mathbb{P}}(\mathfrak{R}^{t}(\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta})\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s))={\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{A}^{t}(\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta})\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s))\,.

(3.6)

The estimates (
3.6
) and (
3.1
) then imply

\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\varepsilon^{2\delta}\,\log{\mathbb{P}}(\mathfrak{R}^{t}(\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}))\geq-\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}}{2{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}^{2}}\,.

(3.7)

Upper bound:

We need the following lemma which shows that
the complementary event

{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)^{c}=\mathbb{\Omega}\setminus{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)
where

{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
is defined in (
1.31
), is
negligible
.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
\delta\in(0,1)
, and
{\mathtt{R}},s,\varepsilon>0
. Then the probability
{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)^{c})
in (
1.31
) is bounded by:

\log{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)^{c})\leq-\frac{{\mathtt{R}}^{2}}{4\|\vec{c}\|_{h^{s}}^{2}}\varepsilon^{-2\delta}+1\ ,\hskip 20.00003pt\mbox{ with }\ \|\vec{c}\|_{h^{s}}\ \mbox{defined in }\ \eqref{norm.c}\,.

(3.8)

Proof.

By (
1.21
) and (
1.41
),

\left\lVert(\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})\right\rVert_{H_{0}^{s}\times{\dot{H}}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}=2\left\lVert\zeta_{0}^{\omega}\right\rVert_{H^{s+\frac{1}{4}}}^{2}=2\,\varepsilon^{2}\,\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}X_{j}^{\omega}\hskip 20.00003pt\mbox{where}\ X_{j}^{\omega}:=c_{j}^{2}|j|^{2s+\frac{1}{2}}\,\varepsilon^{-2}\,|\zeta_{0j}^{\omega}|^{2}.

(3.9)

Since
\zeta_{0j}^{\omega}\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{C}}\big(0,\varepsilon^{2}\frac{c_{j}^{2}}{|j|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big)
(cf. (
3.2
)), we have that
X_{j}^{\omega}\sim\mathscr{E}\big(\frac{1}{c_{j}^{2}|j|^{2s}}\big)
by
1.6
(v). We have to bound the tails of a sum of exponential random variables. Note that

\mathbb{E}[e^{\rho X_{j}^{\omega}}]=1+\frac{c_{j}^{2}|j|^{2s}\rho}{1-c_{j}^{2}|j|^{2s}\rho},\hskip 20.00003pt\mbox{for any}\ \rho<\frac{1}{c_{j}^{2}|j|^{2s}}.

(3.10)

By the Chernoff inequality, the independence of
(X_{j}^{\omega})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
and (
3.10
), for any
0<\rho<\frac{1}{\left\lVert\vec{c}\right\rVert_{h^{s}}^{2}}
(cf. (
1.40
))

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}X_{j}^{\omega}\geq\lambda\Big)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\rho\lambda}\,\mathbb{E}[e^{\rho\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}X_{j}^{\omega}}]\leq e^{-\rho\lambda}\prod_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}\Big(1+\frac{c_{j}^{2}|j|^{2s}\rho}{1-c_{j}^{2}|j|^{2s}\rho}\Big)

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(-\rho\lambda+{\mathop{\sum}}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}\log\Big(1+\frac{c_{j}^{2}|j|^{2s}\rho}{1-c_{j}^{2}|j|^{2s}\rho}\Big)\Big)\,.

(3.11)

Setting
\rho:=(2\left\lVert\vec{c}\right\rVert_{h^{s}}^{2})^{-1}
, we have
1-c_{j}^{2}|j|^{2s}\rho\geq 1/2
for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
and
(
3.11
) and
\log(1+x)\leq x

imply

{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}X_{j}^{\omega}\geq\lambda\Big)\leq\exp\Big(-\frac{\lambda}{2\left\lVert\vec{c}\right\rVert_{h^{s}}^{2}}+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}\frac{c_{j}^{2}|j|^{2s}}{\left\lVert\vec{c}\right\rVert_{h^{s}}^{2}}\Big)=\exp\Big(1-\frac{\lambda}{2\left\lVert\vec{c}\right\rVert_{h^{s}}^{2}}\Big)\,.

(3.12)

Recalling (
1.30
)
and (
3.9
) we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)^{c})

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\lVert(\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})\right\rVert_{H_{0}^{s}\times\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}>{\mathtt{R}}^{2}\,\varepsilon^{2-2\delta}\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}X_{j}^{\omega}\geq\frac{{\mathtt{R}}^{2}\,\varepsilon^{-2\delta}}{2}\Big)\leq\exp\Big(1-\frac{{\mathtt{R}}^{2}\varepsilon^{-2\delta}}{4\left\lVert\vec{c}\right\rVert_{h^{s}}^{2}}\Big)

by the estimate (
3.12
)
with
\lambda={\mathtt{R}}^{2}\varepsilon^{-2\delta}/2
.
∎

Remark 3.4
.

The optimal bound is

\log{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{0}^{c})\leq-\frac{{\mathtt{R}}^{2}}{2\|\vec{c}\|_{h^{s}}^{2}}\varepsilon^{-2\delta}+1
,
as proved in

[
47
, Theorem 5.1]
.

We can now conclude the proof of the upper bound.
Taking
{\mathtt{R}}>2\lambda_{0}\|\vec{c}\|_{h^{s}}{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}^{-1}

and
\varepsilon
sufficiently small,
the probability
{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)^{c})
is much smaller than (
3.1
), in particular

{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)^{c})\leq{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{A}^{t}(\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta})\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s))\,.

(3.13)

Using (
3.5
) and (
3.13
), we find

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\mathfrak{R}^{t}(\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}))

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}(\mathfrak{R}^{t}(\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta})\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s))+{\mathbb{P}}(\mathfrak{R}^{t}(\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta})\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)^{c})

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{A}^{t}(\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta})\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s))+{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)^{c})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:B0c_control}}}{{\leq}}2{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{A}^{t}(\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta})\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s))\,.

(3.14)

The estimates (
3.14
) and
(
3.1
) then imply

\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\varepsilon^{2\delta}\,\log{\mathbb{P}}(\mathfrak{R}^{t}(\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}))\leq-\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}}{2{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}^{2}}.

(3.15)

In conclusion,
the bounds (
3.7
) and (
3.15
) yield the desired result (
3.1
).
∎

4
Rogue waves on nonlinear timescales

In this section we prove Theorem
3.1

for any time
|t|\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{5}{2}(1-\delta)+\kappa}
in item (
i
).
The key step is to define an approximate profile
\eta_{\rm app}(t,x)
which stays close to the water waves
surface
\eta(t,x)
over this time
interval, and whose distribution is
Gaussian,
see properties (
i
)-(
ii
) below.

4.1
The approximate solution

Let us first define the following linear operator.
Given
T>0
,
{\mathtt{s}}\geq 0
and a function
g\in C^{0}([0,T],{\dot{H}^{1}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}))
,
\tau\mapsto g(\tau)
, let

\begin{split}\Upxi(t,g):\dot{H}^{\mathtt{s}}&\longrightarrow C^{0}([0,T],\dot{H}^{\mathtt{s}})\\
v&\longmapsto\Upxi(t,g)[v](x):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}e^{-{\rm i}\int_{0}^{t}{\mathcal{L}}_{j}(I(g(\tau)))\,{\rm d}\tau}\,v_{j}\,e^{{\rm i}jx}\,,\end{split}

(4.1)

where
{\mathcal{L}}_{j}(I)
are the nonlinear frequencies in (
2.24
), and

I(g(\tau))=\big(|g_{k}(\tau)|^{2}\big)_{k\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}

(4.2)

are the actions of
g(\tau)\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T})
,
cf. (
2.23
), and
g_{k}(\tau)
denotes its
k
-th Fourier coefficient.
For each
t
fixed each

{\mathcal{L}}_{j}(I)
is finite by (
2.25
) since
g(\tau)\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T})
. Furthermore

\Upxi(t,g)
is a Fourier multiplier and, since
all the
{\mathcal{L}}_{j}(I)
are real,
it is a unitary transformation from
\dot{H}^{\mathtt{s}}
to itself.

The approximation of the free water waves surface profile
\eta(t,x)
with initial datum

(\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})
that we shall use is

\eta_{\rm app}(t,\cdot):=\sqrt{2}{\rm Re}\,|D|^{\frac{1}{4}}\,u_{\rm app}(t,\cdot)\ ,\hskip 20.00003ptu_{\rm app}(t,x):=\Upxi(t,\zeta_{0}^{\omega})\zeta_{0}^{\omega}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}e^{-{\rm i}t{\mathcal{L}}_{j}(I(\zeta_{0}^{\omega}))}\,\zeta_{0j}^{\omega}\,e^{{\rm i}jx}\,,

(4.3)

where
\zeta_{0}^{\omega}(x)
is the initial datum in (
1.22
).
Note that
\eta(t,x)

and
\eta_{\rm app}(t,x)

have the same initial datum
(\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})
. Remark that

u_{\rm app}(t,x)=\Upxi(t,\zeta_{0}^{\omega})\zeta_{0}^{\omega}
is the solution
of the integrable cubic Birkhoff
normal form
(
2.26
) with random initial datum
\zeta_{0}^{\omega}(x)
in
(
1.22
). It is not clear that it is still a complex Gaussian random variable, since its phases involve also the moduli
(|\zeta_{0j}|)_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
.
We shall prove the following key properties of the approximate solution
u_{\rm app}(t,x)
:

•

(
i
)
Approximation:
the approximate profile

\eta_{\rm app}(t,\cdot)
stays close to the water waves surface
\eta(t,\cdot)

in the sup-norm
\|\ \|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}
,
up to times
|t|\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{5}{2}(1-\delta)+\kappa}
, see Lemma
4.1
.

•

(
ii
)
Preservation of Gaussianity:
for any

(t,x)
the approximate water wave
profile
\eta_{\rm app}(t,x)
is a Gaussian random variable,
see Lemma
4.4
.

We start with the first property (
i
).

Lemma 4.1
.

(Approximation)

Fix
\delta\in(0,1)
and
s\geq N+\frac{5}{4}
with
N
given by
2.4
.
For any
{\mathtt{R}}>0
, any
0<\kappa\ll 1-\delta
, there exist
\varepsilon_{0}:=\varepsilon_{0}({\mathtt{R}},\kappa),{\mathtt{C}}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
, the following holds true.
For any initial datum

(\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})\in{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)
defined in (
1.30
),
the solution
(\eta(t,x),\psi_{0}^{\omega},\psi(t,x))
of the water waves equations (
1.5
) exists up to times
|t|\leq T_{\varepsilon}:=\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)+\kappa}
and

\left\lVert\eta(t,\cdot)-\eta_{\rm app}(t,\cdot)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq{\mathtt{C}}\,{\mathtt{R}}^{4}\,\varepsilon^{1-\delta+\kappa}\ ,\hskip 20.00003pt\forall|t|\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{5}{2}(1-\delta)+\kappa}\,,

(4.4)

where

\eta_{\rm app}(t,x)
is the approximate solution in (
4.3
) (which has the same initial datum
(\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})
).

Proof.

By
2.8
, for any
(\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})\in{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)
the functions

u(t):=u(t,x):=u(t,x;\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})\,,\hskip 10.00002ptz(t):=z(t,x):=z(t,x;\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})=\mathfrak{B}(u(t))u(t)

are well
defined for all times
|t|\leq T_{\varepsilon}
and satisfy (
2.39
).
We have to bound the sup-norm of

\eta(t,\cdot)-\eta_{\rm app}(t,\cdot)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{etaapptilde},\eqref{u0}}}{{=}}\sqrt{2}\,{\rm Re}|D|^{\frac{1}{4}}(u-u_{\rm app})\,.

(4.5)

The key to estimate
u(t,\cdot)-u_{\rm app}(t,\cdot)
is the normal form
2.4
which reduces the problem to comparing the distance between the normal form variable
z(t,\cdot)
and the solution

\Upxi(t,z_{0})z_{0}
of the integrable Birkhoff normal form (
2.26
) with initial datum
z_{0}
.
In view of the invertibility of
\mathfrak{B}(u)
(see
2.4
) and setting

u_{0}(x):=u(t,x)|_{t=0}
and

z_{0}(x):=z(t,x)|_{t=0}
,
by the triangle inequality we have

\displaystyle\|\eta(t)-\eta_{\rm app}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:diffe}}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle\left\lVert u(t)-u_{\rm app}(t)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathfrak{B}^{-1}(u(t))z(t)-z(t)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}

(4.6)

\displaystyle+\left\lVert z(t)-\Upxi(t,z_{0})z_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}

(4.7)

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\Upxi(t,z_{0})z_{0}-\Upxi(t,u_{0})u_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}

(4.8)

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\Upxi(t,u_{0})u_{0}-u_{\rm app}(t)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}\,.

(4.9)

We estimate each term separately.
We anticipate that the worst contribution, the one giving the restriction on the times

|t|\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{5}{2}(1-\delta)+\kappa}
in (
4.4
), comes from (
4.7
).

Estimate of (
4.6
)
.
By (
2.14
) and (
2.39
) we have, with
s_{0}\gg 1
the constant of
2.4

\eqref{u-uapp1}\lesssim\left\lVert u(t)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}\,\left\lVert z(t)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}+1}}\lesssim{\mathtt{R}}^{2}\varepsilon^{2(1-\delta)}\ ,\hskip 10.00002pt\forall|t|\leq T_{\varepsilon}\,.

(4.10)

Estimate of (
4.8
)
.
First we bound

\eqref{u-uapp3}\lesssim\left\lVert\Upxi(t,z_{0})z_{0}-\Upxi(t,u_{0})z_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}+\left\lVert\Upxi(t,u_{0})z_{0}-\Upxi(t,u_{0})u_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}\,.

(4.11)

To estimate the second term in (
4.11
), we use that
\Upxi(t,u_{0})
is a unitary Fourier multiplier,
hence for every
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\left\lVert\Upxi(t,u_{0})z_{0}-\Upxi(t,u_{0})u_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}=\left\lVert z_{0}-u_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}={\left\lVert\mathfrak{B}(u_{0})u_{0}-u_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{phi_est}}}{{\lesssim}}\left\lVert u_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}+1}}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{z.control}}}{{\lesssim}}{\mathtt{R}}^{2}\varepsilon^{2(1-\delta)}\ .

(4.12)

To bound the first term in (
4.11
), we compare different phases: using the explicit expression (
4.1
), we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Upxi(t,z_{0})z_{0}-\Upxi(t,u_{0})z_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|t|\,\Big(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}j^{2}\left|{\mathcal{L}}_{j}(I(z_{0}))-{\mathcal{L}}_{j}(I(u_{0}))\right|^{2}\,|z_{0j}|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{L_lips}}}{{\lesssim}}|t|\,\left\lVert I(z_{0})-I(u_{0})\right\rVert_{{\mathcal{F}}L^{2,1}}\,\left\lVert z_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{2}}}

(4.13)

\displaystyle{\lesssim|t|\left\lVert z_{0}-u_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}(\left\lVert u_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{2}}+\left\lVert z_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{2}})^{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:normeq}}}{{\lesssim}}|t|\left\lVert u_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}+1}}^{4}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{z.control}}}{{\lesssim}}|t|{\mathtt{R}}^{4}\varepsilon^{4(1-\delta)}\,.

(4.14)

Thus by (
4.11
), (
4.12
) and (
4.14
) we have that

\eqref{u-uapp3}\lesssim{\mathtt{R}}^{2}\varepsilon^{2(1-\delta)}+|t|{\mathtt{R}}^{4}\varepsilon^{4(1-\delta)}\lesssim{\mathtt{R}}^{4}\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}(1-\delta)+\kappa}\,,\hskip 10.00002pt\forall|t|\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{5}{2}(1-\delta)+\kappa}\,.

(4.15)

Estimate of (
4.9
)
.
Using the definition

u_{\rm app}(t)=\Upxi(t,\zeta_{0}^{\omega})\zeta_{0}^{\omega}
in
(
4.3
), the term (
4.9
) is estimated similarly to
(
4.8
), replacing the estimate on
\left\lVert z_{0}-u_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}
with

\left\lVert u_{0}-\zeta_{0}^{\omega}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{diffuzeta}}}{{\lesssim}}\left\lVert{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}{(B)}\eta\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{\frac{5}{4}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{prop:action}}}{{\lesssim}}\left\lVert(\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})\right\rVert_{H^{s_{0}}_{0}\times\dot{H}^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\lesssim{\mathtt{R}}^{2}\varepsilon^{2(1-\delta)}\,,

(4.16)

hence obtaining the same kind of estimate

\eqref{u-uapp5}\lesssim{\mathtt{R}}^{4}\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}(1-\delta)+\kappa}\ ,\hskip 20.00003pt\forall|t|\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{5}{2}(1-\delta)+\kappa}.

(4.17)

Estimate of (
4.7
)
.
Writing the equation (
2.22
) as

\dot{z}_{k}=-{\rm i}{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(I(z_{0}))z_{k}-{\rm i}\left({\mathcal{L}}_{k}(I(z))-{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(I(z_{0}))\right)z_{k}+({\mathcal{X}}_{\geq 4}^{+})_{k}\ ,\hskip 10.00002pt\forall k\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}\,,

and using the Duhamel formula, we obtain that

\displaystyle z(t)=

\displaystyle\ \Upxi(t,z_{0})z_{0}-{\rm i}\,\int_{0}^{t}\Upxi(t-\tau,z_{0})\Big[\Big({\mathcal{L}}(I(z(\tau)))-{\mathcal{L}}(I(z_{0}))\Big)\,z(\tau)\Big]\,{\rm d}\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\Upxi(t-\tau,z_{0})({\mathcal{X}}^{+}_{\geq 4})(u,\overline{u},z,\overline{z})(\tau)\,{\rm d}\tau\,,

where we denoted the Fourier multiplier
{\mathcal{L}}(I(z))w:=\sum_{j\neq 0}{\mathcal{L}}_{j}(I(z))w_{j}e^{{\rm i}jx}
.
Then, since
\Upxi(t,\cdot)
is a unitary Fourier multiplier and using (
2.25
) (as in (
4.1
))

\displaystyle\eqref{u-uapp2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\ |t|\,\left\lVert I(z)-I(z_{0})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}([0,t],\mathcal{F}L^{2,1})}\left\lVert z\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}([0,t],\dot{H}^{2})}+|t|\,\left\lVert({\mathcal{X}}^{+}_{\geq 4})(u,\overline{u},z,\overline{z})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}([0,t],\dot{H}^{1})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{action_est},\eqref{X_4_1}}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle\ |t|^{2}\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\left\lVert u(\tau)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}^{5}\,\left\lVert z\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}([0,t],\dot{H}^{2})}+|t|\,\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\left\lVert u(\tau)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}^{4}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{z.control}}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle\ |t|^{2}\,{\mathtt{R}}^{6}\,\varepsilon^{6(1-\delta)}+|t|\,{\mathtt{R}}^{4}\,\varepsilon^{4(1-\delta)}\lesssim{\mathtt{R}}^{6}\,\varepsilon^{1-\delta+\kappa},\hskip 20.00003pt\forall|t|\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{5}{2}(1-\delta)+\kappa}.

(4.18)

Estimate (
4.4
) follows from
eqs.

4.10
,
4.11
,
4.11
and
4.18
.
∎

4.2
Preservation of Gaussianity

We now prove the second important property, namely that

u_{\rm app}(t,x)
is a complex Gaussian random variable.
This is nontrivial since each nonlinear frequency

{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(I(\zeta_{0}^{\omega}))
in (
2.24
) depends on all the
infinitely
many
actions
I_{j}^{\omega}:=|\zeta_{0j}^{\omega}|^{2}

and therefore two random variables
{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(I(\zeta_{0}^{\omega}))
,
{\mathcal{L}}_{k^{\prime}}(I(\zeta_{0}^{\omega}))
are unlikely to be independent.

4.3
below generalizes
[
37
, Lemma 4.2]
and

[
56
, Lemma 4.2]
, which deal with the much simpler case where
\mathcal{L}_{k}(I)=I_{k}
for any
k
.
This diagonal structure of the normal frequencies allows for a much simpler proof of independence.
We therefore proceed differently with a new probabilistic argument.
We start with a useful lemma about conditional expectation.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
and
let
X
be a random vector in a probability space
(\mathbb{\Omega},\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}})
taking values in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and let
f:\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a bounded Borel-measurable function. Define the function

g(y):=\mathbb{E}[f(X,y)]\,,\hskip 20.00003pty\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\,.

If
\mathcal{G}\subseteq\mathcal{F}
is a
\sigma
-algebra such that
\sigma(X)
is independent of
{\mathcal{G}}
and
Y
is a
\mathcal{G}
-measurable random vector taking values in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
, then

\mathbb{E}[f(X,Y)\mid\mathcal{G}]=g(Y)\,.

Proof.

This is proved in
[
62
, (10.17)]
.
∎

Using
4.2
we deduce the following result.

Lemma 4.3
.

(Preservation of gaussianity)

Let
\zeta^{\omega}\equiv(\zeta_{j}^{\omega})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
be a sequence of independent complex Gaussian random variables

\zeta_{j}^{\omega}\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{C}}(0,\sigma_{j}^{2})\hskip 10.00002pt\text{with}\hskip 10.00002pt\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}j^{2}\sigma_{j}^{2}<\infty\,.

(4.19)

Then, for any time
t\in\mathbb{R}
fixed,

\left(\zeta_{j}^{\omega}\,e^{-{\rm i}t{\mathcal{L}}_{j}(I(\zeta^{\omega}))}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
where each
{\mathcal{L}}_{j}(I(\zeta^{\omega}))
is defined by (
2.23
)-(
2.24
), is a sequence of independent complex Gaussians with
\zeta_{j}^{\omega}\,e^{-{\rm i}t{\mathcal{L}}_{j}(I(\zeta^{\omega}))}\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{C}}(0,\sigma_{j}^{2})
.

Proof.

For each
k\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
, we write

\zeta_{k}^{\omega}=R_{k}^{\omega}e^{{\rm i}\phi_{k}^{\omega}}

where
R_{k}^{\omega}
is Rayleigh distributed and
\phi_{k}^{\omega}\sim\mathscr{U}([0,2\pi])
are independent, as well as independent from
(R_{j}^{\omega})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0,k\}}
and
(\phi_{j}^{\omega})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0,k\}}
. For simplicity of notation we drop the dependence on
\omega
.
For fixed
t
, the random variable

X_{k}:=-t{\mathcal{L}}_{k}(I(\zeta))
, is a.s. finite by condition (
4.19
). We note that
X_{k}
only depends on the sequence
(R_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
, but is otherwise independent of
(\phi_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
.
Our goal is to show that

\varphi_{k}(X_{k}):=\phi_{k}+X_{k}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{(mod}\ 2\pi)

has a uniform distribution in
[0,2\pi]
, and that it is independent of
(R_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
and
(\varphi_{j}(X_{j}))_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0,k\}}
.

Uniform distribution:
Using the tower property (cf.
1.4
(i)), we compute the characteristic function

\mathbb{E}[e^{{\rm i}\lambda\varphi_{k}(X_{k})}]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[e^{{\rm i}\lambda\varphi_{k}(X_{k})}\mid X_{k}]]=\mathbb{E}[h_{k}(X_{k})]

(4.20)

where
h_{k}(r):=\mathbb{E}[e^{{\rm i}\lambda\varphi_{k}(r)}]
and in the second equality we applied
4.2
with
{\mathcal{G}}=\sigma(X_{k})
, exploiting that
X_{k}
and
\phi_{k}
are independent.
Using the translation invariance of the uniform distribution, it is clear that
\varphi_{k}(r)
has a uniform distribution in
[0,2\pi]
and thus
h_{k}(r)=h_{k}(0)
, which yields the desired result.

Independence:

To prove the independence of an arbitrary finite number of different r.v.
R_{k_{1}},\ldots,R_{k_{n}}
and
\varphi_{j_{1}}(X_{j_{1}}),\ldots,\varphi_{j_{m}}(X_{j_{m}})
such that
k_{r}\neq k_{s}
if
r\neq s
,
j_{r}\neq j_{s}
if
r\neq s
and
j_{r}\neq k
for all
r
, it is sufficient to show the following factorization property of the characteristic function

\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left({\rm i}\lambda_{0}\varphi_{k}(X_{k})+{\rm i}\sum_{s=1}^{n}\lambda_{s}R_{k_{s}}+{\rm i}\sum_{s=1}^{m}\lambda_{s+n}\varphi_{j_{s}}(X_{j_{s}})\right)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[e^{{\rm i}\lambda_{0}\phi_{k}}\right]\,\prod_{s=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{{\rm i}\lambda_{s+n}\,\phi_{j_{s}}}\right]\,\prod_{s=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{{\rm i}\lambda_{s}R_{k_{s}}}\right].

(4.21)

To prove (
4.21
),
consider the sigma-algebra generated by this finite collection of random variables, namely

\mathcal{G}=\sigma(R_{k_{1}},\ldots,R_{k_{n}},X_{j_{1}},\ldots,X_{j_{m}})\,.

Using the independence of
(\phi_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
from
\mathcal{G}
, as well as
1.4
(i) and (ii),
4.2
yields

\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left({\rm i}\lambda_{0}\varphi_{k}(X_{k})+{\rm i}\sum_{s=1}^{n}\lambda_{s}R_{k_{s}}+{\rm i}\sum_{s=1}^{m}\lambda_{s+n}\varphi_{j_{s}}(X_{j_{s}})\right)\right]\\
=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left({\rm i}\lambda_{0}\varphi_{k}(X_{k})+{\rm i}\sum_{s=1}^{n}\lambda_{s}R_{k_{s}}+{\rm i}\sum_{s=1}^{m}\lambda_{s+n}\varphi_{j_{s}}(X_{j_{s}})\right)\Big|\,\mathcal{G}\right]\right]\\
=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left({\rm i}\lambda_{0}\varphi_{k}(X_{k})+{\rm i}\sum_{s=1}^{m}\lambda_{s+n}\varphi_{j_{s}}(X_{j_{s}})\right)\Big|\,\mathcal{G}\right]\exp\left({\rm i}\sum_{s=1}^{n}\lambda_{s}R_{k_{s}}\right)\right]\\
=\mathbb{E}\left[h(X_{k},X_{j_{1}},\ldots,X_{j_{m}})\,\exp\left({\rm i}\sum_{s=1}^{n}\lambda_{s}R_{k_{s}}\right)\right]

(4.22)

where

h(r_{0},r_{1},\ldots,r_{m}):=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left({\rm i}\lambda_{0}\varphi_{k}(r_{k})+{\rm i}\sum_{s=1}^{m}\lambda_{s+n}\varphi_{j_{s}}(r_{j_{s}})\right)\right].

Using the invariance of the (circular) uniform distribution under translations and the independence of
(\phi_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
among each other, we find

h(r_{0},r_{1},\ldots,r_{m})=h(0,\ldots,0)=\mathbb{E}\left[e^{{\rm i}\lambda_{0}\phi_{k}}\right]\,\prod_{s=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{{\rm i}\lambda_{s+n}\,\phi_{j_{s}}}\right].

(4.23)

Using (
4.22
), (
4.23
), and the independence of
(R_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
, we obtain

\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left({\rm i}\lambda_{0}\varphi_{k}(X_{k})+{\rm i}\sum_{s=1}^{n}\lambda_{s}R_{k_{s}}+{\rm i}\sum_{s=1}^{m}\lambda_{s+n}\varphi_{j_{s}}(X_{j_{s}})\right)\right]\\
=\mathbb{E}\left[h(X_{k},X_{j_{1}},\ldots,X_{j_{m}})\,\exp\left({\rm i}\sum_{s=1}^{n}\lambda_{s}R_{k_{s}}\right)\right]\\
=\mathbb{E}\left[e^{{\rm i}\lambda_{0}\phi_{k}}\right]\,\prod_{s=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{{\rm i}\lambda_{s+n}\,\phi_{j_{s}}}\right]\,\prod_{s=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{{\rm i}\lambda_{s}R_{k_{s}}}\right]

thereby establishing (
4.21
).
∎

Lemmata
4.3
and
3.2
directly imply the following result.

Lemma 4.4
.

For any random initial datum
(\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})
as in (
1.13
), for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
x\in\mathbb{T}
,

1.

the
approximate solution

u_{\rm app}(t,x)
defined in (
4.3
) is
a complex Gaussian random variable;

2.

the real profile

\eta_{\rm app}(t,x)
in (
4.3
)
is Gaussian
\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{R}}\big(0,\varepsilon^{2}{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}^{2})

and it has the form

\eta_{\rm app}(t,x)=\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{\pi}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}c_{j}R_{j}^{\omega}\,\cos(\theta_{j}^{\omega}(t)+jx)\,,

(4.24)

where
(R_{j}^{\omega})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
are time-independent i.i.d. Rayleigh r.v.
\sim\mathscr{R}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}})
and
(\theta_{j}^{\omega}(t))_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
are i.i.d. uniform r.v.
\sim\mathscr{U}([0,2\pi])
. In addition
R_{j}^{\omega}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}\theta_{j^{\prime}}^{\omega}(t)
for any
j,j^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
.

Proof.

By (
3.2
), the complex Gaussian r.v.
(\zeta_{0j}^{\omega})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
in (
1.22
) fulfill the assumptions of Lemma
4.3
, hence

\zeta_{0j}^{\omega}e^{-{\rm i}t{\mathcal{L}}_{j}(I(\zeta_{0}^{\omega}))}\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{C}}\Big(0,\varepsilon^{2}\tfrac{c_{j}^{2}}{|j|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)
.
Thus item 1 follows by the bullets below
1.5
.

Formula (
4.24
) follows from (
4.3
) by writing

\zeta_{0j}^{\omega}e^{-{\rm i}t{\mathcal{L}}_{j}(I(\zeta_{0}^{\omega}))}=|\zeta_{0j}^{\omega}|e^{{\rm i}\theta_{j}(t)}

with

\theta_{j}^{\omega}(t)=\phi_{j}^{\omega}-t{\mathcal{L}}_{j}(I(\zeta_{0}^{\omega}))
, i.e.

\eta_{\mathrm{app}}(t,x)={\rm Re}\,\sqrt{2}|D|^{\frac{1}{4}}u_{\mathrm{app}}(t,x)=\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{\pi}}\,\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}\,c_{j}\underbrace{\frac{|j|^{\frac{1}{4}}}{\varepsilon c_{j}}|\zeta_{0j}^{\omega}|}_{=:R_{j}^{\omega}}\,\cos(\theta_{j}^{\omega}(t)+jx),

where

R_{j}^{\omega}
are independent Rayleigh r.v.
\sim\mathscr{R}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}})
by the last bullet
below
1.5
, and
(\theta_{j}^{\omega}(t))_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
are i.i.d. uniform r.v.
\sim\mathscr{U}([0,2\pi])
by
4.3
.
∎

In order to derive Large Deviation Principles involving Rayleigh-distributed random variables,
the following result in
[
37
, Section 2]
is useful.

Lemma 4.5

(
[
37
]
)
.

Suppose that
(R_{j}^{\omega})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
are i.i.d. Rayleigh r.v.
\sim\mathscr{R}(1/\sqrt{2})
, and let

(c_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
be the sequence in (
1.14
).
Then for any fixed
\lambda_{0},\delta>0
, we have that

\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}\epsilon^{2\delta}\log{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}c_{j}R_{j}^{\omega}\geq\lambda_{0}\epsilon^{-\delta}\Big)=-\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}}{\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}c_{j}^{2}}\ .

Using Lemmata
4.4
and
4.5
, we are ready to prove our main theorem for timescales
|t|\lesssim\varepsilon^{-\frac{5}{2}(1-\delta)+\kappa}
.

Proof of Theorem
3.1
-(
i
).

Let
s\geq\underline{s}:=N+\frac{5}{4}
(with
N
given by
2.4
) and

{\mathtt{R}}>\max\left(\lambda_{0}\underline{C}^{-1},\ 2\lambda_{0}\|\vec{c}\|_{h^{s}}{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}^{-1}\right):={\mathtt{R}}_{0}

(4.25)

where
\underline{C}
is as in (
2.37
).
The first condition guarantees that the

\left\{\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\eta(t,x)\geq\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}\right\}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)
is empty, the second one is used in (
4.31
).

By
4.1
,
provided
\varepsilon
is small enough,
any initial datum
(\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})\in{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)\equiv{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
gives rise to a water waves solution defined
for any
|t|\leq T_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)+\kappa}
, whose wave profile
is uniformly approximated by

\eta_{\rm app}(t,x)
defined in (
4.3
), as stated in (
4.4
).
By the triangle inequality

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\eta_{\rm app}(t,x)-\left\lVert\eta(t)-\eta_{\mathrm{app}}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\eta(t,x)\leq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\eta_{\rm app}(t,x)+\left\lVert\eta(t)-\eta_{\mathrm{app}}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}

(4.26)

so the wave surface
\eta(t,x)
becomes large
if the approximate profile

\eta_{\rm app}(t,x)
does.

For any
\lambda>0
we define the events

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\rm app}(\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\Big\{\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\eta_{\rm app}(t,x)\geq\lambda\Big\}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\,,\hskip 20.00003pt\mathcal{C}(\lambda):=\left\{\left\lVert\eta(t)-\eta_{\mathrm{app}}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\geq\lambda\right\}\,,

(4.27)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\pm}(\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\Big\{\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\eta_{\rm app}(t,x)\pm\left\lVert\eta(t)-\eta_{\mathrm{app}}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\geq\lambda\Big\}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\,.

In the sequel we denote
{\mathcal{C}}(\lambda)^{c}:=\mathbb{\Omega}\setminus{\mathcal{C}}(\lambda)
the complementary event. We fix

\underline{{\mathtt{C}}}:=2{\mathtt{C}}\lambda_{0}^{-1}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{where}\ {\mathtt{C}}>0\mbox{ is defined in estimate \eqref{upxi_error20}}\,.

(4.28)

Upper bound:

Using the second inequality in (
4.26
), and recalling (
4.27
), we get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\Big\{\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\eta(t,x)\geq\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}\Big\}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\Big)\leq

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{P}}\left(\mathcal{D}_{+}\left({\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{P}}\left(\mathcal{D}_{+}\left({\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}}\right)\cap\mathcal{C}\left({\underline{{\mathtt{C}}}\,{\mathtt{R}}^{6}\,\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta+\kappa}}\right)^{c}\,\right)

\displaystyle+{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mathcal{D}_{+}\left({\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}}\right)\cap\mathcal{C}\left(\underline{{\mathtt{C}}}\,{\mathtt{R}}^{6}{\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta+\kappa}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{P}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{\rm app}\left({\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}}\,(1-\underline{{\mathtt{C}}}\,{\mathtt{R}}^{6}\,\varepsilon^{\kappa})\right)\right)+{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mathcal{C}\left(\underline{{\mathtt{C}}}\,{\mathtt{R}}^{6}{\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta+\kappa}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{terrone}}}{{\leq}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{\rm app}\left({\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}}\,(1-\underline{{\mathtt{C}}}\,{\mathtt{R}}^{6}\,\varepsilon^{\kappa})\right)\right)+\exp\Big(-\frac{{\mathtt{R}}^{2}\,\varepsilon^{-2\delta}}{4\left\lVert\vec{c}\right\rVert_{h^{s}}^{2}}+1\Big)

(4.29)

because by
4.1
and (
4.28
), for any
|t|\leq\varepsilon^{-\frac{5}{2}(1-\delta)+\kappa}
we have the inclusion

\mathcal{C}\left(\underline{{\mathtt{C}}}\,{\mathtt{R}}^{6}{\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta+\kappa}}\right)\subseteq{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)^{c}\,.

We now prove that the first term in (
4.29
) is dominant. Indeed,
by (
4.24
), and recalling (
4.27
), we get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{\rm app}\left(\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}\,(1-\underline{{\mathtt{C}}}\,{\mathtt{R}}^{6}\,\varepsilon^{\kappa})\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}c_{j}R_{j}^{\omega}\geq\sqrt{\pi}\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{-\delta}(1-\underline{{\mathtt{C}}}{\mathtt{R}}^{6}\,\varepsilon^{\kappa})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big({-\frac{\varepsilon^{-2\delta}\lambda_{0}^{2}}{2{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}^{2}}+o(\varepsilon^{-2\delta})}\Big)

(4.30)

by
4.5
with
\epsilon\leadsto\varepsilon(1-\underline{{\mathtt{C}}}{\mathtt{R}}^{6}\,\varepsilon^{\kappa})^{-1/\delta}
, since each
R_{j}^{\omega}\sim\mathscr{R}(1/\sqrt{2})
, with
{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}
in (
1.16
).

In conclusion (
4.29
) and (
4.30
) imply that,
since
{\mathtt{R}}>2\lambda_{0}\|\vec{c}\|_{h^{s}}{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}^{-1}
, for
\varepsilon\to 0^{+}
,

{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\Big\{\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\eta(t,x)\geq\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}\Big\}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\Big)\leq\exp\Big(-\varepsilon^{-2\delta}\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}}{2{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}^{2}}+o(\varepsilon^{-2\delta})\Big)\,

(4.31)

and we deduce the upper bound estimate
in (
1.26
)

\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\varepsilon^{2\delta}\log{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\Big\{\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\eta(t,x)\geq\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}\Big\}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\Big)\leq-\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}}{2{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}^{2}}\,,\hskip 10.00002pt{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}\ \mbox{in \eqref{upsigma}.}

Lower bound:
Using the first inequality in (
4.26
), and recalling (
4.27
), we estimate

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\Big\{\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\eta(t,x)\geq\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}\Big\}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\Big)\geq

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{P}}\left(\mathcal{D}_{-}\left({\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}}\right)\right)

(4.32)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{P}}\left(\mathcal{D}_{-}\left({\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}}\right)\cap\mathcal{C}\left({\underline{{\mathtt{C}}}\,{\mathtt{R}}^{6}\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta+\kappa}}\right)^{c}\,\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathcal{A}_{\rm app}\left({\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}}\,(1+\underline{{\mathtt{C}}}{\mathtt{R}}^{6}\,\varepsilon^{\kappa})\right)\cap\mathcal{C}\left(\underline{{\mathtt{C}}}\,{\mathtt{R}}^{6}{\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta+\kappa}}\right)^{c}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{P}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{\rm app}\left({\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}}\,(1+\underline{{\mathtt{C}}}{\mathtt{R}}^{6}\,\varepsilon^{\kappa})\right)\right)-{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mathcal{C}\left(\underline{{\mathtt{C}}}\,{\mathtt{R}}^{6}{\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta+\kappa}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:events},\eqref{terrone}}}{{\geq}}

\displaystyle\ {\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\eta_{\rm app}(t,x)\geq\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}(1+\underline{{\mathtt{C}}}\,{\mathtt{R}}^{6}\varepsilon^{\kappa})\Big)-\exp\Big(-\frac{{\mathtt{R}}^{2}\,\varepsilon^{-2\delta}}{4\left\lVert\vec{c}\right\rVert_{h^{s}}^{2}}+1\Big)\,.

By
4.4
we get

\varepsilon^{-1}\eta_{\rm app}(t,0)\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{\mathbb{R}}(0,{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}^{2})
with
{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}
in (
1.16
),
and thus, for
\varepsilon
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}

\displaystyle\Big(\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\eta_{\rm app}(t,x)\geq\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}(1+\underline{{\mathtt{C}}}\,{\mathtt{R}}^{6}\varepsilon^{\kappa})\Big)\geq{\mathbb{P}}\left(\eta_{\rm app}(t,0)\geq\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}(1+\underline{{\mathtt{C}}}\,{\mathtt{R}}^{6}\varepsilon^{\kappa})\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt={\mathbb{P}}\left(\varepsilon^{-1}\eta_{\rm app}(t,0)\geq\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{-\delta}(1+\underline{{\mathtt{C}}}\,{\mathtt{R}}^{6}\varepsilon^{\kappa})\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{asintail}}}{{\gtrsim}}\frac{\varepsilon^{\delta}{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}}{\lambda_{0}\,\sqrt{2\pi}}\,\exp\Big(-\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}\varepsilon^{-2\delta}}{2{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}^{2}}(1+\underline{{\mathtt{C}}}\,{\mathtt{R}}^{6}\varepsilon^{\kappa})^{2}\Big).

(4.33)

The estimates (
4.32
), (
4.33
) imply that, provided
{\mathtt{R}}>2\lambda_{0}\|\vec{c}\|_{h^{s}}{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}^{-1}

{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\Big\{\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\eta(t,x)\geq\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}\Big\}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\Big)\geq\exp\Big(-\varepsilon^{-2\delta}\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}}{2{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}^{2}}+o(\varepsilon^{-2\delta})\Big)\,\hskip 10.00002pt{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}\ \mbox{in \eqref{upsigma}}\,,

and we deduce the lower bound estimate in (
1.26
)

\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\varepsilon^{2\delta}\log{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\Big\{\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\eta(t,x)\geq\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}\Big\}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\Big)\geq-\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}}{2{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}^{2}}\,.

5
Rogue waves on optimal timescales

In this section we prove Theorems
3.1

for any time
|t|\leq\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)+\kappa}
in item (
ii
). Over this extended (optimal) timescale,
the profile in (
4.3
) is not an approximation of the surface water wave anymore, and we
need a more refined one.
The idea is to use
as approximate solution

\eta_{\rm app2}(t,\cdot)
in (
5.1
),
whose
phases are evaluated along the solution
z(t;\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})
of (
2.15
) and not along the solution of the cubic Birkhoff normal form (
2.26
), which preserves the actions,
cf. (
4.3
).
The introduction of
nonlinearly time-evolving phases
improves the time-validity of the approximation, cf.
Lemma
5.1
. This is sufficient to prove the upper bound of the Large Deviation Principle of
5.2
. Conversely, a new issue arises: we can no longer guarantee that the new approximate solution

\eta_{\rm app2}(t,x)

is still a Gaussian random variable. A new strategy is needed to prove
the lower bound of the Large Deviation Principle of

5.7
.
This is addressed by the construction in
5.3
.
Finally, in Section
5.4
we prove
1.2
.

5.1
The new approximate solution
and the upper bound

We define

\displaystyle\eta_{\rm app2}(t,\cdot)

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{2}{\rm Re}\,|D|^{\frac{1}{4}}\,u_{\rm app2}(t,\cdot)\ ,

(5.1)

\displaystyle u_{\rm app2}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=\Upxi\big(t,z(\cdot;\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})\big)\zeta_{0}^{\omega}={\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}e^{-{\rm i}\int_{0}^{t}{\mathcal{L}}_{j}(I(z(\tau;\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega}))){\rm d}\tau}\,\zeta_{0j}^{\omega}\,e^{{\rm i}jx}\,,

where
\zeta_{0}^{\omega}(x)
is the initial datum in (
1.22
),
with Fourier coefficients

\zeta_{0j}^{\omega}=|\zeta_{0j}^{\omega}|e^{{\rm i}\phi_{j}^{\omega}}
,

\Upxi(t,\cdot)
is the linear unitary operator in (
4.1
),
and
z(t;\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})
is defined in (
2.38
) (recall it solves the equation (
2.15
)).
The approximate wave profile (
5.1
) can be written as

\eta_{\rm app2}(t,x)={\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{\pi}}}\,\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}c_{j}R_{j}^{\omega}\,\cos\left(\vartheta_{j}(t;\omega)+jx\right)

(5.2)

where
c_{j}
are defined in (
1.14
),

•

the amplitudes

R_{j}^{\omega}:={\dfrac{|j|^{\frac{1}{4}}|\zeta_{0j}^{\omega}|}{\varepsilon c_{j}}}
are i.i.d Rayleigh r.v.
\sim\mathscr{R}(1/\sqrt{2})
(by (
3.2
));

•

nonlinear phases are

\vartheta_{j}(t;\omega):=-\int_{0}^{t}{\mathcal{L}}_{j}(I(z(\tau;\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega}))){\rm d}\tau+\phi_{j}^{\omega}\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{(mod}\ 2\pi)\,.

(5.3)

Note that since

z(\tau;\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})
depends on the initial datum
(\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})
in a very complex and nonlinear way, the phases
\vartheta_{j}(t;\omega)
are
generally not uniformly distributed in the desired timescale nor independent among themselves or from the
R_{j}^{\omega}
’s. This stands in contrast
with the previous approximation
\eta_{\rm app}(t,x)
in (
4.3
).

However, a key property is that

\eta_{\rm app2}(t,x)
approximates the water waves surface

\eta(t,x)
in the
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

on the whole time interval
|t|\leq\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)+\kappa}
, as proved in the next lemma.

Lemma 5.1
.

(Approximation 2)

Fix
\delta\in(0,1)
and
s\geq N+\frac{5}{4}
with
N
given by
2.4
.
For any
{\mathtt{R}}>0
, any
0<\kappa\ll 1-\delta
, there exist
\varepsilon_{0}:=\varepsilon_{0}({\mathtt{R}},\kappa),{\mathtt{C}}_{2}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
, the following holds true.
For any initial datum

(\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})\in{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)
in (
1.30
),
the solution
(\eta(t,x),\psi(t,x))
of the water waves equations (
1.5
) exists up to times
|t|\leq T_{\varepsilon}:=\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)+\kappa}
, as well as the approximate solution
\eta_{\rm app2}(t,\cdot)

in (
5.1
), and satisfy

\left\lVert\eta(t,\cdot)-\eta_{\rm app2}(t,\cdot)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\left\lVert u(t,\cdot)-u_{\rm app2}(t,\cdot)\right\rVert_{H^{1}}\leq{\mathtt{C}}_{2}\,{\mathtt{R}}^{4}\,\varepsilon^{1-\delta+\kappa}\ ,\hskip 20.00003pt\forall|t|\leq T_{\varepsilon}\,.

(5.4)

Proof.

By
2.8
, for any
(\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})\in{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)
the functions

u(t):=u(t,x):=u(t,x;\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})\,,\hskip 10.00002ptz(t):=z(t,x):=z(t,x;\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})=\mathfrak{B}(u(t))u(t)\,,

are well
defined for all times
|t|\leq T_{\varepsilon}
and satisfy (
2.39
).
Then the approximate solutions

u_{\rm app2}(t,x)
and
\eta_{\rm app2}(t,x)
in (
5.1
) are well defined for all

|t|\leq T_{\varepsilon}
.
We now estimate

\eta(t,\cdot)-\eta_{\rm app2}(t,\cdot)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{etaapp2},\eqref{u0}}}{{=}}\sqrt{2}\,{\rm Re}|D|^{\frac{1}{4}}\big(u(t,\cdot)-u_{\rm app2}(t,\cdot)\big)\,.

(5.5)

As in
4.1
, the key idea is to use the normal form
2.4
to reduce the problem to comparing the normal form variable
z(t,\cdot)
with the solution
\Upxi(t,z(\cdot))z_{0}
of the integrable Birkhoff normal form (
2.26
), where
z_{0}(x):=z(0,x)
, but now allowing for nonlinearly evolving phases.
Set

u_{0}(x):=u(t,x)|_{t=0}
.
By (
5.5
), Sobolev embedding,
and since
\mathfrak{B}(u)
is invertible (see
2.4
), we estimate

\displaystyle\left\lVert\eta(t,\cdot)-\eta_{\rm app2}(t,\cdot)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\lVert u(t,\cdot)-u_{\rm app2}(t,\cdot)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathfrak{B}^{-1}(u(t,\cdot))z(t,\cdot)-z(t,\cdot)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}

(5.6)

\displaystyle+\left\lVert z(t,\cdot)-\Upxi(t,z)z_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}

(5.7)

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\Upxi(t,z)z_{0}-u_{\rm app2}(t,\cdot)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}\,.

(5.8)

We estimate each contribution separately. Line (
5.6
) is
equal to (
4.6
) which is estimated by
(
4.10
).

Estimate of (
5.8
).
By (
5.1
) and since
\Xi(t,\cdot)
is a unitary Fourier multiplier for every
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\eqref{u-uapp42}\leq\left\lVert z_{0}-\zeta_{0}^{\omega}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}\leq\left\lVert z_{0}-u_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}+\left\lVert u_{0}-\zeta_{0}^{\omega}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{0209:1212},\eqref{u0-zeta0}}}{{\lesssim}}{\mathtt{R}}^{2}\varepsilon^{2(1-\delta)}\ .

(5.9)

Estimate of (
5.7
).

Write

z(t):=\Upxi(t,z)v(t)
; by variation of constants, using
(
2.22
), (
4.1
), the variable

v(t)
satisfies

{\partial}_{t}v=\Upxi(t,z)^{-1}{\mathcal{X}}_{\geq 4}^{+}(u,\overline{u},z,\overline{z})\,,\hskip 10.00002ptv(0)=z_{0}\,.

By the unitarity of
\Upxi(t,z)
and estimate (
2.19
) we bound

\left\lVert{\partial}_{t}v(t)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}\lesssim\left\lVert u(t)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}^{4}
,
implying

\eqref{u-uapp22}=\left\lVert v(t)-z_{0}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}\lesssim|t|\sup_{|\tau|\leq t}\left\lVert u(\tau)\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}^{4}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{z.control}}}{{\lesssim}}{\mathtt{R}}^{4}\varepsilon^{1-\delta+\kappa}\ ,\hskip 10.00002pt\forall|t|\leq T_{\varepsilon}\ .

(5.10)

Estimate (
5.4
) follows from
(
4.10
),
(
5.9
),
(
5.10
).
∎

With this approximation lemma, we can readily prove the upper bound of
3.1

(ii)
.

Proposition 5.2

(
LDP: Upper-bound on optimal timescale).

There exists
\underline{s}>0

such that
for any
\lambda_{0}>0
,
\delta\in(0,1)
, and
s\geq\underline{s}
there exists
{\mathtt{R}}_{0}>0
and for any
{\mathtt{R}}\geq{\mathtt{R}}_{0}
, any
0<\kappa\ll 1-\delta
, the following holds true. There exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
,
for any random initial data
(\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})\in{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)
in (
1.30
),
the solution
(\eta(t,x),\psi(t,x))
of the water waves equations (
1.5
) exists up to times
|t|\leq\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)+\kappa}
, and satisfies
the upper bound

\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\varepsilon^{2\delta}\log{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\{\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\eta(t,x)\geq\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}\right\}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)\right)\leq-\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}}{{2{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}^{2}}}\,,

(5.11)

with
{\boldsymbol{\upsigma}}
in (
1.16
).

Proof.

Argue as in the proof of Theorem
3.1

(i)
(upper bound), replacing
\eta_{\rm app}(t,x)
with

\eta_{\rm app2}(t,x)
, the constant
{\mathtt{C}}
with

{\mathtt{C}}_{2}
,
using Lemma
5.1
in place of Lemma
4.1
and using that

\left\lVert\eta_{\rm app2}(t,\cdot)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:eta2notg}}}{{\leq}}\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{\pi}}\sum_{j\neq 0}c_{j}\,R_{j}^{\omega}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{where}\hskip 10.00002ptR_{j}^{\omega}\sim\mathscr{R}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\right)\,,

from which it follows that

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\eta_{\rm app2}(t,x)\geq\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}(1-{\mathtt{C}}_{2}{\mathtt{R}}^{4}\,\varepsilon^{\kappa})\right)\leq{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}c_{j}R_{j}^{\omega}\geq\sqrt{\pi}\,\lambda_{0}\varepsilon^{-\delta}(1-{{\mathtt{C}}_{2}}{\mathtt{R}}^{4}\,\varepsilon^{\kappa})\Big)\,.

Then
4.5

implies (
5.11
).
∎

Proving a sharp lower bound over the desired timescale
|t|\leq\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)+\kappa}
requires much more work.
The key point to have a rogue wave form at time
t
is that plenty of the phases

\vartheta_{j}(t;\omega)
in (
5.3
)
align at zero, so that, in the expression (
5.2
) evaluated at
x=0
,

\cos\left(\vartheta_{j}(t;\omega)\right)\simeq 1
and one can exploit again
4.5
.
We can guarantee that, given an arbitrary time

|t|\leq\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)+\kappa}
, a finite but large number of phases (
5.2
) align at zero
via a random fixed point theorem, that is the content of the next
Section

5.2
. The probability that random initial phases coincide with the fixed point is zero, so we construct an event of quasi-synchronized phases by perturbing around the fixed point. An additional difficulty is to estimate the measure of this event.
It is in this point that we need to keep track of the Lipschitz dependence of
z(t,\cdot)\equiv z(t;\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})
on the initial datum.

5.2
Random fixed point

For any integer
{\mathtt{N}}\geq 1

we consider the low/high frequency projectors

\displaystyle\Pi_{\leq{\mathtt{N}}}:\dot{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C})\longrightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C})\ ,\hskip 10.00002ptf(x)\longmapsto\Pi_{\leq{\mathtt{N}}}f(x):={\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}}\sum_{0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}}f_{j}\,e^{{\rm i}jx}\ ,

(5.12)

\displaystyle\Pi_{>{\mathtt{N}}}:\dot{L}^{2}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C})\longrightarrow L^{2}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C})\,,\hskip 10.00002ptf(x)\longmapsto\Pi_{>{\mathtt{N}}}f(x):={\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}}\sum_{|j|>{\mathtt{N}}}f_{j}\,e^{{\rm i}jx}\,,

so that
{\rm Id}=\Pi_{\leq{\mathtt{N}}}+\Pi_{>{\mathtt{N}}}
.
The truncation parameter
{\mathtt{N}}

will be linked to
\varepsilon
in (
5.41
).

Partially randomized initial data.

For any
\vec{\phi}\in\mathbb{R}^{2{\mathtt{N}}}
we define the

partially randomized
initial data

\displaystyle

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\widetilde{\eta}}_{0}^{\omega}(x;\vec{\phi}):={\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{\pi}}}\sum_{0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}}c_{j}\,R_{j}^{\omega}\,\cos\left(\phi_{j}+jx\right)+\underbrace{{\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{\pi}}}\sum_{|j|>{\mathtt{N}}}c_{j}\,R_{j}^{\omega}\,\cos\left(\phi_{j}^{\omega}+jx\right)}_{=\Pi_{>{\mathtt{N}}}{\widetilde{\eta}}_{0}^{\omega}}

(5.13)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\widetilde{\psi}}_{0}^{\omega}(x;\vec{\phi}):={\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{\pi}}}\sum_{0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}}d_{j}\,R_{j}^{\omega}\,\sin\left(\phi_{j}+jx\right)+\underbrace{{\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{\pi}}}\sum_{|j|>{\mathtt{N}}}d_{j}\,R_{j}^{\omega}\,\sin\left(\phi_{j}^{\omega}+jx\right)}_{=\Pi_{>{\mathtt{N}}}{\widetilde{\psi}}_{0}^{\omega}}

where
c_{j}
are the coefficients defined in (
1.14
),
d_{j}=c_{j}|j|^{-\frac{1}{2}}
, and

•

(R_{j}^{\omega})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
are i.i.d. Rayleigh random variables
\sim\mathscr{R}(1/\sqrt{2})
;

•

(\phi_{j}^{\omega})_{|j|>{\mathtt{N}}}
are i.i.d. random variables with a uniform distribution

\sim\mathscr{U}([0,2\pi])
;

•

the random variables
(R_{j}^{\omega})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}

and
(\phi_{j}^{\omega})_{|j|>{\mathtt{N}}}
are independent.

Note that in (
5.13
)
the high Fourier modes are fully random, while low Fourier modes have random moduli but
deterministic
phases
\vec{\phi}
.

If
\vec{\phi}^{\omega}=(\phi_{j}^{\omega})_{0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}}
are i.i.d. random variables
\sim\mathscr{U}([0,2\pi])
, independent of
(R_{j}^{\omega})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
and
(\phi_{j}^{\omega})_{|j|>{\mathtt{N}}}
, then

({\widetilde{\eta}}_{0}^{\omega},{\widetilde{\psi}}_{0}^{\omega})(x;\vec{\phi}^{\omega})=(\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})(x)

(5.14)

where the right-hand side denotes the fully randomized initial datum in (
1.13
).
In particular, for any
\vec{\phi}\in\mathbb{R}^{2{\mathtt{N}}}
,

\displaystyle\big\|({\widetilde{\eta}}_{0}^{\omega}(\cdot,\vec{\phi}),{\widetilde{\psi}}_{0}^{\omega}(\cdot,\vec{\phi}))\big\|_{H^{s}_{0}\times\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}

\displaystyle=2\,\varepsilon^{2}\,\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}c_{j}^{2}(R_{j}^{\omega})^{2}|j|^{2s}

(5.15)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt=2\,\varepsilon^{2}\,\!\!\sum_{0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}}\!\!c_{j}^{2}(R_{j}^{\omega})^{2}|j|^{2s}+\big\|(\Pi_{>{\mathtt{N}}}{\widetilde{\eta}}_{0}^{\omega},\Pi_{>{\mathtt{N}}}{\widetilde{\psi}}_{0}^{\omega})\big\|_{H^{s}_{0}\times\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}=\left\lVert(\eta_{0}^{\omega},{\psi}_{0}^{\omega})\right\rVert_{H^{s}_{0}\times\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\,.

As a result, the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)
in (
1.31
) only depends on the variables

(\vec{R}^{\omega},\Pi_{>{\mathtt{N}}}{\widetilde{\eta}}_{0}^{\omega},\Pi_{>{\mathtt{N}}}{\widetilde{\psi}}_{0}^{\omega})\hskip 10.00002pt\text{where}\hskip 10.00002pt\vec{R}^{\omega}:=(R_{j}^{\omega})_{0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{in}\ \eqref{eq:part_rand_init}\,.

Random nonlinear phase operator.

At any time
|t|\leq\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)+\kappa}
, we define the operator

\begin{split}{\mathcal{T}}:{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)\times\mathbb{R}^{2{\mathtt{N}}}&\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2{\mathtt{N}}}\,,\\
(\omega,\vec{\phi})&\longmapsto({\mathcal{T}}_{j}(\omega,\vec{\phi}))_{0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}}\,,\end{split}

(5.16)

where, for any
0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}
,

{\mathcal{T}}_{j}(\omega,\vec{\phi}):=\int_{0}^{t}{\mathcal{L}}_{j}(I(z(\tau;\omega,\vec{\phi})))\,{\rm d}\tau\,,\hskip 20.00003ptz(t;\omega,\vec{\phi}):=\Upsilon^{t}\big({\widetilde{\eta}}_{0}^{\omega}(\cdot,\vec{\phi}),{\widetilde{\psi}}_{0}^{\omega}(\cdot,\vec{\phi})\big)\,,

(5.17)

and
\Upsilon^{t}
is defined in (
2.38
). The domain
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)
in (
5.16
) is the
restriction

({\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s),{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}},{\widetilde{{\mathbb{P}}}})
of the probability space
(\mathbb{\Omega},\mathcal{F},{\mathbb{P}})
given by the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the random moduli and the high-Fourier modes, i.e.

{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}:=\sigma(\vec{R}^{\omega},\Pi_{>{\mathtt{N}}}{\widetilde{\eta}}_{0}^{\omega},\Pi_{>{\mathtt{N}}}{\widetilde{\psi}}_{0}^{\omega})\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)\subseteq\mathcal{F}\,,\hskip 20.00003pt{\widetilde{{\mathbb{P}}}}(F):={\mathbb{P}}(F)\,,\hskip 10.00002pt\forall F\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}\,.

(5.18)

The mapping
{\mathcal{T}}
is well defined since, for any
\omega\in{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)
,

\big\|({\widetilde{\eta}}_{0}^{\omega}(\cdot,\vec{\phi}),{\widetilde{\psi}}_{0}^{\omega}(\cdot,\vec{\phi}))\big\|_{H^{s}_{0}\times\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{1909:1520}}}{{=}}\left\lVert(\eta_{0}^{\omega},{\psi}_{0}^{\omega})\right\rVert_{H^{s}_{0}\times\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\leq{\mathtt{R}}\varepsilon^{1-\delta}

and so
2.8
guarantees that the functions

{\widetilde{u}}(t):={\widetilde{u}}(t,x;{\widetilde{\eta}}_{0}^{\omega}(\cdot,\vec{\phi}),{\widetilde{\psi}}_{0}^{\omega}(\cdot,\vec{\phi}))\,,\hskip 10.00002ptz(t;\omega,\vec{\phi})=\Upsilon^{t}\big({\widetilde{\eta}}_{0}^{\omega}(\cdot,\vec{\phi}),{\widetilde{\psi}}_{0}^{\omega}(\cdot,\vec{\phi})\big)=\mathfrak{B}({\widetilde{u}}(t)){\widetilde{u}}(t)\,,

are well
defined for all times
|t|\leq T_{\varepsilon}
and satisfy (
2.39
).

The next crucial proposition guarantees the existence of a random fixed point for
{\mathcal{T}}
, and studies properties of the event of random phases being close to the fixed point. Recall that, at the random fixed point, the nonlinear phases (
5.3
) vanish, and a rogue wave forms.

The following proposition contains all the results to prove the lower bound (
5.40
).

Proposition 5.3

(
Random fixed point).

Let
{\mathtt{N}}\in\mathbb{N}
,
\delta\in(0,1)
and
s\geq N+\frac{5}{4}
.
For any
{\mathtt{R}}>0
, any

0<\kappa\ll 1-\delta
, there exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
, any

|t|\leq T_{\varepsilon}:=\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)+\kappa}
, the random nonlinear phase operator
{\mathcal{T}}
defined in (
5.16
)–(
5.17
)
satisfies the following properties:

(i)

Lipschitz property.
For any
\omega\in{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)
, the map
{\mathcal{T}}(\omega,\cdot)\colon\mathbb{R}^{2{\mathtt{N}}}\to\mathbb{R}^{2{\mathtt{N}}}
is Lipschitz, and, for any
0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}
,

\big|{\mathcal{T}}_{j}(\omega,\vec{\phi})-{\mathcal{T}}_{j}(\omega,\underline{\vec{\phi}})\big|\leq C_{2}{\mathtt{N}}\,{\mathtt{R}}^{2}\,\varepsilon^{2(1-\delta)}\,e^{C_{\rm lip}|t|}\,\|\vec{\phi}-\underline{\vec{\phi}}\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\,,\hskip 10.00002pt\forall\,\vec{\phi},\underline{\vec{\phi}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2{\mathtt{N}}}\,.

(5.19)

(ii)

Measurability.
For any
\vec{\phi}\in\mathbb{R}^{2{\mathtt{N}}}
, the map
{\mathcal{T}}(\cdot,\vec{\phi})\colon({\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s),{\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}})\to(\mathbb{R}^{2{\mathtt{N}}},{\mathcal{B}}(\mathbb{R}^{2{\mathtt{N}}}))
is measurable.

(iii)

Fixed point.

There exists a random fixed point
\vec{\phi}^{\ast,\omega}=(\phi^{\ast,\omega}_{j})_{0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}}
of
{\mathcal{T}}
,

{\mathcal{T}}(\omega,\vec{\phi}^{\ast,\omega})=\vec{\phi}^{\ast,\omega}\,,

(5.20)

which is a random variable in the restricted probability space
(\mathbb{\Omega},{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}},{\widetilde{{\mathbb{P}}}})
defined in (
5.18
).

Moreover, the fixed point
\vec{\phi}^{\ast,\omega}

satisfies the following properties:

(a)

If
\vec{\phi}^{\omega}=(\phi_{j}^{\omega})_{0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}}
are i.i.d. uniform random variables
\sim\mathscr{U}([0,2\pi])
independent of
(R_{j}^{\omega})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
and
(\phi_{j}^{\omega})_{|j|>{\mathtt{N}}}
,
then, for any
\alpha\in(0,\pi)
, the set

\mathcal{N}(\alpha):=\Big\{\omega\in\mathbb{\Omega}\mid|\phi_{j}^{\omega}-\phi_{j}^{\ast,\omega}|<\alpha\hskip 10.00002pt\forall\ 0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}\Big\}

(5.21)

is
{\mathcal{F}}
-measurable and

{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{N}(\alpha))=\left(\frac{\alpha}{\pi}\right)^{2{\mathtt{N}}}
.

(b)

Stability.

For any
\omega\in\mathcal{N}(\alpha)\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)
we have

\left|\phi_{j}^{\omega}-{\mathcal{T}}_{j}(\omega,\vec{\phi}^{\omega})\right|<2\,\alpha\,{\mathtt{N}}\,e^{C_{\rm lip}\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)}},\hskip 20.00003pt\forall\ 0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}\,,

(5.22)

where
C_{\rm lip}>0
is the constant in

2.8
-(iv).

(c)

Factorization property.
For any measurable set
{\mathcal{A}}\in{\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}}
(see (
5.18
)),

{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{A}}\cap\mathcal{N}(\alpha))={{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{A}})\,{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{N}(\alpha))}=\left(\frac{\alpha}{\pi}\right)^{2{\mathtt{N}}}{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{A}})\,.

(5.23)

Remark 5.4
.

The domain in (
5.16
) is restricted to (
5.18
) in order to guarantee
that the first
2{\mathtt{N}}
uniformly distributed random phases
\vec{\phi}^{\omega}
are independent from
the random fixed point
\vec{\phi}^{\ast,\omega}
(constructed in
5.3
), which is
generated
only by the random variable
(\vec{R}^{\omega},\Pi_{>{\mathtt{N}}}{\widetilde{\eta}}_{0}^{\omega},\Pi_{>{\mathtt{N}}}{\widetilde{\psi}}_{0}^{\omega})
.
This is the key to ensure the crucial factorization property in
5.3
(
c
).

Remarkably, the factorization property (
5.23
) holds even though

\mathcal{N}(\alpha)
and
{\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}}
are not independent.
This property will play a key role in establishing the sharp lower bound
for the probability of rogue wave formation in

5.7
.
In particular, one has

{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{N}(\alpha))=\left(\frac{\alpha}{\pi}\right)^{2{\mathtt{N}}}
.
Since
\left(\frac{\alpha}{\pi}\right)^{2{\mathtt{N}}}\to 0
as
{\mathtt{N}}\to+\infty
,
it is important to choose
{\mathtt{N}}={\mathtt{N}}(\varepsilon)\sim\varepsilon^{-\gamma}

as in (
5.41
) below, which ensures that the measure of the set

\mathcal{N}(\vec{\phi}^{\ast,\omega};\alpha)
does not vanish too rapidly as

\varepsilon\to 0^{+}
.

Corollary 5.5
.

(Phase synchronization for the approximate solution)

Let
{\mathtt{N}}\in\mathbb{N}
,
\delta\in(0,1)
and
s\geq N+\frac{5}{4}
.
For any
{\mathtt{R}}>0
, any

0<\kappa\ll 1-\delta
, there exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
, any

|t|\leq T_{\varepsilon}:=\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)+\kappa}
,
the following holds.
Let
\alpha\in(0,\pi)
such that

2\,\alpha\,{\mathtt{N}}\leq\varepsilon\,e^{-C_{\rm lip}\varepsilon^{-3(1-\delta)}}.

(5.24)

Then, for any
\omega\in\mathcal{N}(\alpha)\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)

(the set

\mathcal{N}(\alpha)
is defined in (
5.21
)),
the first
2{\mathtt{N}}
-phases
\vartheta_{j}(t;\omega)

of the approximate wave profile

\eta_{\rm app2}(t,x)

in (
5.2
)

synchronize
at
x=0
, i.e.

|\vartheta_{j}(t;\omega)|<\varepsilon\,,\hskip 10.00002pt\forall\,0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}\,,\hskip 10.00002pt\vartheta_{j}(t;\omega)\mbox{ in }\eqref{eq:theta}\,.

Consequently

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\eta_{\rm app2}^{\omega}(t,x)\geq{\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{\pi}}}\,(1-\varepsilon)\,\sum_{0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}}c_{j}R_{j}^{\omega}-C{\mathtt{N}}^{-(s-1)}{\mathtt{R}}\,\varepsilon^{1-\delta}.

(5.25)

Proof.

By
5.3

(b)
, for any

\omega\in\mathcal{N}(\alpha)\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s)
the phase synchronization property (
5.22
) occurs.
Recalling the definitions (
5.3
), (
5.17
) and the identity (
5.14
), we have

|\vartheta_{j}(t;\omega)|=\left|\phi_{j}^{\omega}-{\mathcal{T}}_{j}(\omega,\vec{\phi}^{\omega})\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{5.8},\eqref{alpha.N}}}{{\leq}}\varepsilon\hskip 10.00002pt\Rightarrow\hskip 10.00002pt\cos\left(\vartheta_{j}(t;\omega)\right)\geq 1-\varepsilon\ ,\hskip 10.00002pt\forall 0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}\,.

(5.26)

Then we bound

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}}\eta_{\mathrm{app2}}(t,x)\geq\eta_{\rm app2}(t,0)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:eta2notg}}}{{=}}{\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{\pi}}}\sum_{0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}}c_{j}R_{j}^{\omega}\,\cos\left(\vartheta_{j}(t;\omega)\right)+\Pi_{>{\mathtt{N}}}\eta_{\mathrm{app2}}(t,0)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{piccaggio}}}{{\geq}}{\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{\pi}}}(1-\varepsilon)\,\sum_{0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}}c_{j}R_{j}^{\omega}+\Pi_{>{\mathtt{N}}}\eta_{\mathrm{app2}}(t,0)\,.

(5.27)

To bound the last term, we note that for any
\omega\in{\mathcal{B}}_{0}

\displaystyle\big\|\Pi_{>{\mathtt{N}}}\eta_{\mathrm{app2}}^{\omega}(t,\cdot)\big\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{etaapp2}}}{{\lesssim}}\left\lVert\Pi_{>{\mathtt{N}}}\zeta_{0}^{\omega}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{zeta0}}}{{\lesssim}}\left\lVert(\Pi_{>{\mathtt{N}}}\eta_{0}^{\omega},\Pi_{>{\mathtt{N}}}\psi_{0}^{\omega})\right\rVert_{H_{0}^{\frac{3}{4}}\times\dot{H}^{\frac{5}{4}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\mathtt{N}}^{-(s-1)}\,\left\lVert(\eta_{0}^{\omega},\psi_{0}^{\omega})\right\rVert_{H_{0}^{s}\times\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{cB0p}}}{{\lesssim}}{\mathtt{N}}^{-(s-1)}\,{\mathtt{R}}\,\varepsilon^{1-\delta}\,.

(5.28)

Estimate (
5.25
) follows by
(
5.28
) and (
5.27
) .
∎

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of
5.3
.

The fixed point will be found by
the random Brouwer fixed point theorem
1.3
. In order to verify its hypotheses, we first prove a deterministic Lipschitz property of the nonlinear phases, Lemma
5.6
. Then, writing the
nonlinear phase operator
{\mathcal{T}}
in (
5.16
)-(
5.17
) as the composition of a continuous and a measurable map as in (
5.37
), we will deduce properties (
i
) and (
ii
) in
5.3
.

Deterministic nonlinear phase operator

\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
.

For any
s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, let

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Y}}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s,{\mathtt{N}}):=\Big\{v

\displaystyle=(\vec{r},{\widetilde{\eta}}_{0,{\mathtt{N}}},{\widetilde{\psi}}_{0,{\mathtt{N}}})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2{\mathtt{N}}}\times{H}^{s}_{0}\times\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}\ |\ \Pi_{\leq{\mathtt{N}}}\,{\widetilde{\eta}}_{0,{\mathtt{N}}}=\Pi_{\leq{\mathtt{N}}}{\widetilde{\psi}}_{0,{\mathtt{N}}}=0\,,

(5.29)

\displaystyle\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{{\mathcal{Y}}}:=\Big(\sum_{0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}}|j|^{2s}c_{j}^{2}\,r_{j}^{2}+\big\|({\widetilde{\eta}}_{0,{\mathtt{N}}},{\widetilde{\psi}}_{0,{\mathtt{N}}})\big\|_{{H}_{0}^{s}\times\dot{H}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\leq{\mathtt{R}}\,\varepsilon^{-\delta}\Big\}

where the constants
c_{j}
are introduced in (
1.14
).
Then we define for any
0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}
the deterministic map

\begin{split}{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}_{j}&:{\mathcal{Y}}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s,{\mathtt{N}})\times\mathbb{R}^{2{\mathtt{N}}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\,,\\
(v,\vec{\phi})&\longmapsto{{\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}}}_{j}(v,\vec{\phi})=\int_{0}^{t}{\mathcal{L}}_{j}\big(I(z(\tau;v,\vec{\phi})\big)\,{\rm d}\tau\,,\qquad z(\tau;v,\vec{\phi}):=\Upsilon^{t}(({\widetilde{\eta}}_{0},{\widetilde{\psi}}_{0})(\cdot;v,\vec{\phi}))\,,\end{split}

(5.30)

where
\Upsilon^{t}
is introduced in (
2.38
) and,
for any
(v,\vec{\phi})\in{\mathcal{Y}}(\varepsilon,\delta,{\mathtt{R}},s,{\mathtt{N}})\times\mathbb{R}^{2{\mathtt{N}}}
,

\begin{split}{\widetilde{\eta}}_{0}(x;v,\vec{\phi})&:=\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{\pi}}\sum_{0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}}c_{j}\,r_{j}\,\cos\left(\phi_{j}+jx\right)+\varepsilon\,{\widetilde{\eta}}_{0,{\mathtt{N}}}(x)\\
{\widetilde{\psi}}_{0}(x;v,\vec{\phi})&:=\frac{\varepsilon}{\sqrt{\pi}}\sum_{0<|j|\leq{\mathtt{N}}}d_{j}\,r_{j}\,\sin\left(\phi_{j}+jx\right)+\varepsilon\,{\widetilde{\psi}}_{0,{\mathtt{N}}}(x)\end{split}

(5.31)

where
d_{j}=c_{j}|j|^{-\frac{1}{2}}
.

Lemma 5.6

(
